The new test asserts that with `None`, a `milestoned` event by a verified human CODEOWNER for the *matching* title **still returns `None`** — i.e. the omitted argument is strict, not permissive. **If this test is ever "fixed" to make an omitted argument permissive, the fix is the bug.**
- **`test_expected_milestone_none_still_allows_label_authorization`** — the other half of F27: with `None`, a CODEOWNER `labeled` event authorizes normally. Without this pair, a later reader cannot tell "ignore milestone events" from "ignore everything."
- **`test_milestone_title_match_is_byte_for_byte`** (F28) — a table of near-miss titles that must all fail: case difference, leading/trailing whitespace, em dash vs. hyphen, and a **prefix** (`"v0.7.0"` against `"v0.7.0 — <suffix>"`). The prefix case is the one that matters, because `bootstrap/edit_issue.sh` prefix-matches by design and a coder reading both may reasonably think that is the house convention.
- `test_bot_actor_after_codeowner_does_not_erase_authorization` — pins the `continue`-not-`return None` semantics
- `test_non_list_events_returns_none`

**Named acceptance checks the coder must perform and record (not unit tests):**
- **§0.6 gap 5** — observe one live `milestoned` issue-event payload and confirm the `milestone` object's fields. If `number` is present, implement title equality **anyway** as AM-4 binds it, and raise the discrepancy to `technical-design`; do not substitute number equality on your own judgment.
- **§0.6 gap 6** — **moved to §6.2 in revision 3, and it is no longer a one-command read.** AM-14 scopes it as a **live mutating re-apply on a throwaway surface, run by the coder under a bot identity, never on a live candidate issue**, which makes it a gate-slice acceptance check rather than a primitive one. Revision 2 listed it here while §0.6 pointed at §6.2; the two are reconciled in §6.2's favour, which is where AM-14 also places it.
- **Amendment §0 gap 3** — confirm live that #1643's `needs-ai` label actor is `ScottThurlow`. The architect flagged this as the load-bearing ergonomic valve in AM-1 and asked that it be confirmed once before S2 merges rather than assumed from §2.6.

### 6.2 `test_select_work_candidates.py` — the gate

**Ordering parity** — the nine cases ported per §4.2, each with a trusted author.

**Trust and authorization** (each drives the CLI with a stubbed `gh`)
- **`test_untrusted_author_with_worker_applied_needs_ai_is_not_a_candidate`** — the core #1539 fix, VF-3's exact shape
- `test_untrusted_author_with_codeowner_applied_label_is_a_candidate`
- `test_untrusted_author_with_codeowner_applied_milestone_is_a_candidate`
- **`test_human_proxy_bot_authored_issue_is_gated_without_marker`** — author `scottthurlow-claude[bot]`, ordinary title: the #1539/#1340/#1356/#1540 shape from §2.6
- **`test_human_proxy_bot_labelling_does_not_authorize`** — AD-6's named asymmetry: `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` applied `needs-ai` **and** the milestone on an untrusted-authored issue → still gated. This is the case the ADR says "must be tested as its own case."
- **`test_worker_self_labelled_worker_authored_issue_is_rejected`** — #1678's shape: author and both label/milestone actors are `hos-worker-hos[bot]`, title carries no marker → gated
- `test_codeowner_applied_label_releases_an_issue_the_bot_later_relabelled` — #1643's shape
- `test_baseline_repair_blocked_issue_is_selectable_with_zero_events_calls` — the one live machine-filing site: author `hos-worker-hos[bot]`, title `[BLOCKED] inner-loop tests failing on HumanOversightSystem — diagnose and fix`, `needs-ai`+`priority:critical`, milestone set → selectable, and the `gh` stub records exactly one API call
- `test_codeowner_authored_issue_costs_zero_extra_api_calls` — AD-3's cost claim, asserted rather than assumed
- `test_roster_listed_author_is_selectable`
- `test_copilot_bot_authored_issue_is_gated`
- `test_issue_body_claiming_codeowner_identity_is_gated` (FR5)
- **`test_pull_request_records_are_excluded`** (DEV-1)

**AM-12's binding condition on DEV-1 — the bounce loop must be proved intact, not assumed.** DEV-1 is approved *conditional on this test*, and the condition has teeth:

- **`test_bounced_draft_pr_still_reaches_the_worker_and_blocks_the_skip`** — a bounced draft PR carrying the dispatch label, with the candidate list **empty**, must (a) still be surfaced to the worker through `bin/hos-cron`'s PR path and (b) still prevent the `#1395` no-actionable-work early skip.

  I read that path this revision and it is satisfiable by construction, which the coder should know before writing the test: `_OPEN_PR_NUMS` is populated from `gh api repos/<slug>/pulls?state=open` (`bin/hos-cron:1049-1050`) — **a different endpoint from the issues query the gate replaces** — and the bounce detection at `:1085` reads that PR's own `draft` flag and labels (`:1078-1086`). The skip condition at `:1158` requires `-z "$_OPEN_PR_NUMS"` **independently of** `-z "$_GATE_CANDIDATES"`. So excluding `pull_request` records from the *issues* list cannot affect PR surfacing at all. The test pins that independence rather than discovering it.

  **If the test cannot be made to pass, DEV-1 is withdrawn and PR records stay in** — escalate to the architect. Do **not** adjust the skip logic to make it pass; that would be repairing the gate's blast radius by widening it.

**Prefix-correctness (AM-19) — the property the whole ruling rests on. NEW in revision 4, and it is FOUR tests, not one.**

§2.3 D5.1 states prefix-correctness as a conjunction of four independent limbs. **A single test named `test_prefix_correctness` would report "prefix-correctness broke" and leave the next reader to find out which limb** — the same objection §6.3 already makes to a combined regex for G11's four conditions, and it applies with more force here because this is the property a CRITICAL finding was closed on.

- **`test_rank_is_computed_before_any_authorization_check`** — limb (a) and the **direct P-1 regression test**. A fixture of 40 records in `created desc` order with a single `priority:critical` untrusted-authored record at position 38, authorized by a CODEOWNER `labeled` event, and `--max-authorization-checks 3`. **The critical record MUST be emitted**, and the `gh` stub must record that its events call was among the **first three** made. Under revision 3's algorithm this test fails; under revision 4's it passes at any ceiling ≥ 1. **This is #1539's own shape, and the fixture's comment must say so**, because a later reader deleting it as "an arbitrary ordering test" is deleting the regression test for the defect that produced this revision.
- **`test_ranking_reads_only_the_list_payload`** — limb (a). The rank function is called with records whose every field except `labels` is a sentinel that raises on access; ranking must still succeed. A rank that reaches for anything the events call would supply can change between D-rank and emit.
- **`test_rank_number_key_is_a_total_order_on_the_candidate_set`** — limb (b). Two records at the same rank never compare equal; issue numbers are unique, so the key is total and no tie is broken non-deterministically. Assert the emitted order is identical across repeated runs of a shuffled input.
- **`test_free_filters_run_before_ranking`** — limb (c). A PR record and a `needs-human` record, both `priority:critical`, must be absent from the ranked set entirely — **not merely absent from the output**. Assert via the rank function's call log, not the emitted list: a filter applied *after* ranking produces identical stdout and a different set of paid checks.
- **`test_no_record_is_admitted_after_a_stop_including_a_trusted_one`** — limb (d), and **it is the test that pins the specific thing revision 3 got wrong.** A fixture where the record at rank position 30 is **trusted-authored** (zero-cost) and the ceiling is exhausted at position 12. **The trusted record MUST NOT be emitted.** Revision 3's D5.1 would have emitted it, because it skipped only remaining *untrusted* records. Amendment 3's risk register names this as failure mode (iii) — *"the sufficiency stop being read as the gate may stop whenever it likes"* — and it breaks prefix-correctness **silently**, with every membership-based test still green.
- **`test_emitted_list_is_a_prefix_of_the_complete_walk`** — the composed property, as a **differential** test rather than an assertion about internals: run the same fixture twice, once with the default bounds and once with bounds high enough that `complete=yes`, and assert `bounded_output == unbounded_output[:len(bounded_output)]` **byte for byte**. This is the one test that would catch a limb nobody thought to enumerate.

**The three bounds and their interactions (AM-22) — NEW in revision 4:**

- **`test_step_c_paginates`** — the `gh` stub asserts `--paginate` is present on the list call and that concatenated page arrays are normalised (two pages of 100 → 200 records, no `][` in the parsed payload). **Include a page boundary that is `"]\n["` rather than `"]["`**, because that is the case `require_human_approval.py`'s own comment records as the reason a naive `replace` is wrong.
- **`test_list_page_bound_truncates_loudly`** (F33) — 6 full pages offered, 5 consumed; asserts `WARN candidate-list-truncated`, `unevaluated:list-truncated`, `complete=no`, **and exit 0**.
- **`test_cost_ceiling_stops_the_walk_and_warns`** (F32) — asserts `WARN authorization-cost-ceiling-reached`, `unevaluated:cost-ceiling`, `complete=no`, exit 0, and `auth_checks == 100`.
- **`test_sufficiency_stop_is_quiet_but_reported`** (F31) — asserts `unevaluated:sufficient` and `complete=no` are present **and that no `WARN` line is emitted.** The routine stop must not train the operator to ignore WARNs.
- **`test_pagination_and_ceiling_interaction_is_ordered_not_arbitrary`** — **the panel's §3(c) item 2, answered in code rather than in prose.** 500 records across 5 pages, 3 `priority:critical` records among the last 100 (i.e. the oldest, the region a single page would have dropped), ceiling 100. Assert **all three criticals are evaluated**. This is the constructive answer to *"is there a population where the combination is worse than either alone?"*: **pagination widens the ranked set, the ceiling truncates the ranked tail, and because ranking sits between them the widening can only ever move a high-priority record INTO the evaluated region, never out of it.** Under revision 3 those three records were unreachable at any cap; under revision 4 they are evaluated first.
- **`test_max_list_pages_is_not_a_flag`** — the asymmetry in §2.2: the cost ceiling may be lowered, the page bound may not, because one truncation is ordered and the other is not.

**The `complete=yes` invariant (AM-20) — NEW in revision 4. Tested as an invariant, not as a happy path.**

- **`test_complete_is_yes_only_when_nothing_was_skipped`** — a **parameterised sweep** over every path that can end a walk early (sufficiency, cost ceiling, list truncation, and all three in combination) asserting `complete=no` in each, plus the unbounded case asserting `complete=yes`. **This is the failure the AM-20/AM-21 pair exists to make impossible** — the panel's §3(c) item 3 is *"is there a state where `complete=yes` is emitted and the determination is not complete?"* — so it is tested exhaustively over the state space rather than at one point in it.
- **`test_summary_counts_are_internally_consistent`** — `scanned == evaluated + unevaluated`, `evaluated == eligible + gated`, `authorized <= eligible`, in every fixture in this file. A property-style assertion applied to all cases, not a separate fixture.
- **`test_unevaluated_records_are_not_counted_as_gated`** — the core of AM-20. A ceiling-truncated walk with 5 determinations-of-held and 40 never-reached records must report `gated=5 unevaluated=40`, **not** `gated=45`. Counted as gated, the line mislabels a **starved** issue as *"held for human authorization"* — a false statement about a person's obligation.
- **`test_unevaluated_tokens_never_appear_in_the_gated_reasons_breakdown`** — AM-20 point 3, asserted as an **absence** on the `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED reasons:` line.
- **`test_all_gated_block_declares_incompleteness`** — F30's new fourth line: when `eligible==0`, `gated>0` **and** `complete=no`, the `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED INCOMPLETE` line is present and names `<U>`. Amendment 3's failure mode (iv) is *"`complete=no` being dropped as a field nobody parses"*; this test is what makes that a red suite rather than a quiet regression.
- **`test_sufficiency_stop_and_all_gated_are_mutually_exclusive`** — AM-21's structural claim, pinned: no fixture can produce both, because the stop requires `eligible ≥ 1` and the block requires `eligible == 0`.

**AM-24's authorization lines — NEW in revision 4:**

- **`test_authorized_count_distinguishes_d5_from_d4`** — a fixture with one trusted-authored candidate and one CODEOWNER-authorized candidate → `eligible=2 authorized=1`.
- **`test_each_d5_authorized_candidate_emits_its_actor`** — one `AUTHORIZED issue=#<n> actor=<login> via=<labeled|milestoned>` line per such candidate, on stderr. **In the S2 era these lines are the entire machine-readable record that a human's unaided judgement was the only judgement in the loop** (§2.3 Step F) — and they do **not** satisfy FR17, which is deferred to S5 and is not testable here because the condition it warns about is universal.

**AM-13 visibility (F30) — the gate must be loud when it holds everything:**
- **`test_all_gated_emits_the_distinct_held_line`** — `eligible=0, gated>0` produces the `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` block on stderr, with the count, the reason tokens, and the issue numbers
- **`test_empty_milestone_does_not_emit_the_held_line`** — `eligible=0, gated=0` produces the summary line and **nothing else**. This is the test that gives the previous one its meaning: a token emitted in both cases distinguishes nothing.
- `test_held_line_issue_list_is_bounded` — more than 20 gated issues renders `(+k more)` rather than an unbounded line
- `test_all_gated_still_exits_zero` — the held condition is never an exit code and never an error
- `test_gate_never_creates_an_issue` — the `gh` stub records no `POST` to `/issues` under any input (AM-13 point 3; also FR-scope: §5's "never writes to GitHub")

**Fail-closed** — one test per row **F1–F33** of §3, each asserting the exit code, empty stdout where applicable, and the stderr reason token. **Three qualifications, all new in revision 4:**

- **F17's row additionally asserts the `milestone-object-absent` diagnostic** (AM-18, §1.6): the record is skipped *and* the line appears on stderr. An "unreachable" branch tested only for its silence is a branch nobody can prove fired.
- **F15 has no test, because F15 no longer exists** — it is a stub pointing at F31/F32 (§3). A coder counting rows should get **32 live rows**, not 33.
- **F25, F30 and F26 do not live in this file.** F25 and F30's `$LOG_PREFIX` half are `tests/automation/test_hos_cron.py`'s (see §6's test-homes table); **F26 is not a behavioural test at all** and §6's preamble says why rather than implying one exists.

**Fail-closed loaders (AM-28(e)) — §1.3.1's table, one test per cell, NEW in revision 4.** The four propagation paths are tested **individually**, not as a group: `codeowners_humans`, `load_trusted_apps`, `load_bot_accounts` and `load_trusted_requesters` each get a file-absent case (→ empty, no exception) and a present-but-unreadable case (→ propagates). **Plus one test the table exists to make possible:** `test_no_loader_catches_bare_exception` — a source-level assertion that `requester_trust.py` contains no `except Exception` and no bare `except:`. That single line, written once, would convert all four propagating failures into silent empties at a stroke — **and for `load_bot_accounts` an empty result is an empty denylist, which promotes every bot to "human"**. It is the most consequential line a coder could write in this module, and a source assertion is the only thing that catches it, because the behavioural tests would all still pass on a *readable* file.

**Named acceptance check the coder must perform and record (not a unit test) — §0.6 gap 6, the decisive live check (AM-14, scoped and binding).**

The premise under E-5 — that re-applying an already-present label emits no new `labeled` event — has been probed read-only as far as it can be (AF-7: 78 issues, 0 duplicates, 0 above one event page, max 17), and no read-only scan of any width can go further, because absence of a duplicate may reflect absence of the *attempt*. The decisive check is a **live mutating re-apply**, and its scope is part of the requirement, not a formality:

- **What:** from the acting identity, apply a label the issue already carries, then read that issue's event list — **paginated, and from the newest end** — for a second `labeled` event with no intervening `unlabeled`.
- **Where: a throwaway surface only** — a scratch issue created for the purpose, or a scratch label on an already-closed issue. **Never on a live candidate issue.** After S2, a stray `unlabeled`/`labeled` pair by a human account on a real issue is **indistinguishable from a cutover authorization act**: the probe would write the very signal the gate reads, before the gate exists to make it legible. Contaminating the evidence base of an authorization mechanism in order to test that mechanism is the kind of self-defeating experiment that is obvious only after it has been run.
- **Who: the `coder`, under a bot identity** — not the human, and not the worker on live state. The behaviour under test is a property of the GitHub API, not of the actor, so a bot-actor probe answers it at strictly lower cost and with no human time.
- **Status: a confirmation, not a precondition.** No ruling depends on the outcome, because §2.7.4's instruction is worded to be correct either way. If a bare re-apply *does* emit a fresh event, the second act becomes unnecessary and the human's burden halves; AM-14's lazy-drain default is unaffected either way. **Report the result on #1539 whichever way it lands** — a gap that has been closed should stop being carried as open.

**Anti-knob (AD-13)** — *updated in revision 4 for the fifth flag and the redefined ceiling*
- **`test_parser_exposes_exactly_five_flags`** — renamed from `…four_flags`: `--repo`, `--milestone`, `--label`, `--max-candidates`, `--max-authorization-checks`
- `test_no_repo_root_flag` / `test_no_exclude_label_flag` / **`test_no_max_list_pages_flag`** (§2.2's asymmetry: the ordered truncation may be lowered, the unordered one may not)
- `test_no_environment_variable_grants_trust` — a sweep setting `TRUSTED_REQUESTERS`, `HOS_TRUSTED_APPS`, `HOS_SKIP_GATE`, `BOT_ACCOUNTS=""`, `CODEOWNERS=…` never makes an untrusted record selectable
- `test_label_flag_cannot_widen_trust` — `--label anything` still gates an untrusted-authored record
- **`test_max_authorization_checks_can_only_lower`** — `--max-authorization-checks 10000` clamps to **100** (was 25 through revision 3)
- **`test_max_candidates_can_only_lower_and_never_to_zero`** — `--max-candidates 10000` clamps to 10; `--max-candidates 0` clamps to **1**, not 0. A sufficiency stop of zero would emit an empty list on every cycle while exiting 0 — a gate that does nothing while claiming to have worked
- **`test_lowering_a_bound_cannot_change_which_records_are_evaluated_first`** — the anti-knob property specific to AM-19: for any legal `--max-authorization-checks`, the *set* evaluated is a prefix of the same order. A flag that reordered the walk would be a widening knob wearing a cost-control costume

**No caching (FR7/AD-4)**
- `test_two_consecutive_runs_both_query_live` — no state file is written and the second run makes the same API calls
- `test_authorization_revoked_between_runs_is_not_carried_over`

### 6.3 `test_selection_call_sites.py` — the collapse, **and G11's acceptance mechanism (AM-11: required, not optional)**

This file carries two jobs that revision 1 ran together without saying so. The first is the collapse itself — proving the second implementation is gone. The second is new in revision 2: **it is the mechanism by which `ADR-1542` G11's requirement is satisfied**, and AM-11 makes that mechanism *required*. G11 is superseded rather than merely overlapped (§2.5), which means S2 has taken on G11's acceptance condition; an intention about a prompt string, unpinned by a test, survives exactly until the next person needs a second milestone.

**The collapse**

- `test_next_candidates_jq_is_gone`
- `test_hos_cron_invokes_the_entry_point` / `test_hos_cron_has_no_inline_jq_selection` (no `--jq` on the candidates query, no `$(cat`)
- `test_cron_prompt_invokes_the_entry_point` / `test_cron_prompt_has_no_gh_api_candidate_query` (no `labels=needs-ai` `gh api`, no `--jq`, no `$(`)
- `test_cron_prompt_forbids_improvised_fallback` — the §2.5 "do NOT construct your own query" instruction is present verbatim
- `test_worker_agent_doc_does_not_cite_next_candidates_jq`
- `test_only_one_selection_entry_point_exists` — `select_work_candidates` is referenced from exactly the two call sites plus tests and docs

**G11's four conditions, as one test (AM-11, binding)**

- **`test_cron_prompt_fallback_is_sandbox_allowlistable`** — asserts of the *rendered* Step 2 fallback line, after `_build_prompt`'s `@@MILESTONE_NUMBER@@` substitution, that it contains **no command substitution** (`$(`, backtick), **no variable expansion** (`$`), **no inline `jq`** (`--jq`, `jq -f`, `jq '`), and **no backslash continuation** (a trailing `\`). Those are G11's four conditions verbatim from AM-11, written as four assertions with four distinct failure messages so a regression names which one broke. A single combined regex would report "the fallback is wrong" and leave the next reader to find out how.

  **HOW the test obtains the rendered prompt — specified in revision 4 (AM-28(a), binding).** Revision 3 said "the rendered Step 2 fallback line, after `_build_prompt`'s substitution" and **did not say how a Python test gets that string out of a bash function inside a 2,000-line shell script.** *"An unspecified mechanism in a test that pins a security-relevant string is a test that gets written the easy way"* — and the easy way here is to `read_text()` the prompt **template** and assert against `@@MILESTONE_NUMBER@@`, which passes while proving nothing about the rendered form. **Binding mechanism, and it is already most of the way built:**

  > The test lives in **`tests/automation/test_hos_cron.py`** and uses the existing `CronEnv` harness, which invokes the **real `bin/hos-cron`** as a subprocess against a stubbed world with a `claude` stub on a pinned `PATH`. **The prompt reaches `claude` on stdin** — `_build_prompt | _run_claude` (`bin/hos-cron:1932`, read by me this session). **The stub currently drains stdin to `/dev/null`** (`test_hos_cron.py:102`) and records only its argv and environment. **One line changes: the stub records stdin to a new `HOS_TEST_CLAUDE_PROMPT_LOG` path instead of discarding it**, exactly as it already records its argv to `HOS_TEST_CLAUDE_LOG`. The test then runs a cycle with `HOS_TARGET_MILESTONE_NUMBER` set and asserts G11's four conditions against **the bytes the launcher actually piped to the model**.
  >
  > **This is strictly stronger than unit-testing the bash function**, and it is why the mechanism is worth a paragraph: it exercises substitution, template assembly and the launcher's own quoting in one pass, so a regression anywhere in that chain fails the test. It also needs **no new harness**, no `declare -f` extraction, no sourcing of `hos-cron` into a test shell — all of which are fragile against a script that runs logic at load time — and it introduces no command substitution or heredoc into the test's own shell usage. **The stub extension is a required part of the S2 change set**, not an implementation detail the coder may skip by testing the template instead.

  This is the one test in the plan whose *absence* would leave an accepted ADR item (G11) marked superseded by a change that does not actually satisfy it. **Note what the justification does NOT rest on (AM-28(g), §2.5):** not on the claim that a non-conforming command fails to execute unattended — that premise is unresolved — but on `CLAUDE.md` requiring allowlistability of **every** command in this repo. The test is required either way, and under the `bypassPermissions` reading it is required *more*, because there the failure is silent **execution** rather than silent skipping.

**AM-6 point 3's shipping condition, as tests (`/approve` must stop claiming authorization)**

AM-6 point 3 is *"S2 MUST NOT ship while the repo tells a human that `/approve` authorizes work."* A shipping condition that lives only in prose is a shipping condition nobody can fail. Both artefacts are repo literals, so both belong in this file rather than in §6.2:

- **`test_labels_doc_states_approve_does_not_authorize`** — `docs/LABELS.md`'s `needs-ai` row (or an adjacent note it links) contains the statement that in the S2 era `/approve` **changes no labels and** does **not** authorize autonomous selection (§4.3, §2.7.2). *Wording updated in revision 4: revision 3 asserted "performs a label swap and does not authorize", which is no longer true of the shipped command.*
- **`test_approve_branch_performs_no_label_write`** — **NEW in revision 4, and it is the mechanical form of AM-25.** `.github/workflows/label-swap.yml`'s `/approve` branch (`:73`-`:77`) contains **no `gh issue edit`** and no `--add-label` / `--remove-label`. This is the test that keeps the ruling from being undone by *"someone who reads AM-25's basis as the wording fix rather than the inert one"* — Amendment 3's failure mode (vi), and a live risk precisely because revision 3 argued the opposite for two revisions and stated three reasons for it.
- **`test_label_swap_confirmation_does_not_claim_authorization`** — the `/approve` confirmation body no longer contains the literal `authorized by` (`:75`). This is an *absence* assertion deliberately: the replacement wording is the coder's (§2.7.2 fixes the facts it must carry, not the phrasing), so pinning the new string would be pinning a choice this document did not make, while pinning the old string's absence pins exactly the lie AM-6 forbids.
- **`test_label_swap_confirmation_states_it_does_not_authorize`** — **NEW in revision 4.** The **presence** half, which revision 3 deliberately omitted and which Amendment 3 §4 now asks for: the body contains a "does not authorize" statement, asserted on a load-bearing token rather than the full prose. **This is §9/R4's hole, closed in one direction and left open in the other**: R4 asked whether a replacement could stop claiming authorization while still misleading. Pinning the absence of the lie and the presence of the denial narrows that gap without the architect or me writing the coder's sentence.
- **`test_label_swap_decline_path_is_unchanged`** — **`:79`**'s `/decline` sibling is untouched — its `gh issue edit --add-label wontfix`, its `gh issue close` (`:80`) and its comment (`:81-82`) all survive. *Line number corrected in revision 4: revision 3 cited `:83`* (AM-28(c); re-derived by me, §0.0.3). S2's edit is one command's branch, not a workflow rewrite, and this test says so mechanically.

**AM-25's runbook requirements, as tests — NEW in revision 4.** `docs/OVERSIGHT-RUNBOOK.md` joins the conformance set for the same reason `docs/LABELS.md` did: it is a repo literal, and AM-1 makes a documented approval path that silently does nothing worse than no gate.

- **`test_runbook_intervention_states_the_act_is_from_your_own_account`** — the "Intervening" control 1 passage carries the own-account qualifier.
- **`test_runbook_intervention_forbids_edit_issue_app_human`** — it names `edit_issue.sh --app human` as **not** an approval path. The prohibition must be where the instruction is; a reader following control 1 will not go looking in `docs/LABELS.md` for a caveat.
- **`test_runbook_intervention_covers_the_already_labelled_case`** — the remove-then-re-add case is present.

**AM-27's documentation requirements, as tests — NEW in revision 4.**

- **`test_labels_doc_states_milestone_route_is_forbidden`** — **AM-27 point 2, binding.** `docs/LABELS.md` carries the prohibition on the milestone route **with the Step 0 race as its stated reason**. Assert both: a prohibition with no reason attached is the first thing a later editor removes as fussy. Revision 3 left this rule with **no documentation home and no test** — its only home was H1's clearance text, and **a prohibition whose only home is one-shot clearance text expires the moment the human answers.** This is the rule the design's own failure mode (ix) is about.
- **`test_no_document_uses_the_phrase_cutover_material`** — **AM-27 point 1.** A repo-wide absence assertion over `docs/v0.7.0/**`. The phrase was used eleven times and never defined; retiring a term is only durable if re-introducing it fails.

**AM-26's ship-set requirements, as tests — NEW in revision 4.**

- **`test_new_framework_modules_are_shipped_to_consumers`** — `select_work_candidates.py` and `requester_trust.py` are present in `scripts/framework/framework_consumer_files.txt`.
- **`test_trusted_requesters_roster_is_not_shipped`** — the live `scripts/framework/trusted-requesters.txt` is **absent** from that list. **An absence test with a reason in its docstring**, because Amendment 3 names *"the roster being added for completeness by a later contributor"* as failure mode (v), and the failure it produces — an HOS upgrade silently deleting a consumer's trusted contributors — is invisible from this repo.
- **`test_every_shipped_framework_module_has_its_framework_imports_shipped`** — **AM-26's binding detector, and the one that generalises.** Parse each `scripts/framework/**` entry in the ship list, collect its `scripts.framework.*` imports, and assert each imported module is itself on the list. For this change set that is `select_work_candidates.py` → `requester_trust.py` → `require_human_approval.py`. **The existing guard test checks only that *listed* files exist, so it cannot see a listed file whose dependency is unlisted** — which is exactly how P-4 arose. Without this test P-4 recurs the next time a shipped module grows an import, and it recurs **invisibly from this repo**: the symptom is a consumer's worker going quiet with a `ModuleNotFoundError` that carries no `$LOG_PREFIX` and no `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` token.

**FR8's own verify clause, as a test — NEW in revision 4 (AM-28(e), binding).** FR8 states: *"a test enumerates the selection paths and asserts agreement."* **Revision 3 had no such test** — §6.3 asserted *reference counts* (`test_only_one_selection_entry_point_exists`), which proves there is one implementation, not that the surviving paths **agree**. Those are different claims, and FR8 asks for the second.

- **`test_all_selection_paths_agree_on_an_unapproved_untrusted_issue`** — one fixture (untrusted author, `needs-ai` applied by the worker bot, in-milestone), evaluated through **both** surviving selection paths: the gate's CLI, and `probe.py`'s milestone strategy. **Both must return a negative verdict.** FR8 names three paths; after §4.1's deletion the worker's Step-2 fallback **is** the gate's CLI, which the test must assert rather than assume — so the test also pins that `worker-cron-prompt.md`'s fallback command is character-identical to the one `bin/hos-cron` invokes.
- **Why this matters beyond the requirement:** FR8 is one of the four FRs `ADR-1540` §3's slice table says S2 **closes**. **Closing a requirement whose own verify clause has no test is how FR4 came to be cited zero times** in 1,363 lines — and P-2 is what that looks like after three revisions.

**AM-15's documentation requirements, as tests — new in revision 3.** AM-15 makes two wording requirements binding, and §0.0.2 records why they need pinning rather than prose: `docs/LABELS.md` declares itself doc-only, and "no conformance test" is one of its own acknowledged gaps, so an unpinned sentence there survives exactly until the next tidy-up. Both artefacts are repo literals, so both belong here:

- **`test_labels_doc_states_the_authorizing_act`** — `docs/LABELS.md` carries §2.7.4's instruction in substance: the act is performed from the human's **own GitHub account**, `edit_issue.sh --app human` is named as **not** an approval path, and the **remove-then-re-add** case for an issue that already carries the label is present. Assert the load-bearing tokens, not the full prose — a test that pins every word makes the sentence unmaintainable and will be deleted rather than updated.
- **`test_labels_doc_states_the_event_pair_is_the_authorization_record`** — the registry contains AM-15's answer to the churn misreading (the `unlabeled`/`labeled` pair by the human's own account **is** the authorization record, not an undo-redo). This is the one sentence standing between a future reader and "this looks like noise, let's document a quieter act."
- **`test_labels_doc_states_approve_can_consume_the_additive_act`** — the `/approve` statement carries the *harmful*, not merely *ineffective*, form (AM-15). Paired with `test_labels_doc_states_approve_does_not_authorize` above, which pins the other half.

### 6.4 `test_probe.py` — **no longer "additive only"** (AM-4)

**Revision 1's acceptance criterion — "all fifteen existing tests pass unmodified" — is WITHDRAWN**, and this section is where that shows up as work. §1.6 explains why: behaviour-preservation was the wrong goal, and it is what produced the unsafe default AM-4 removed. **A test that must change is the honest signal that behaviour changed.**

**SIX existing tests must change — five named in §1.6's table, plus a sixth added in revision 4 by AM-28(d).** Restated here as the test-plan obligation, because a PR that quietly rewrites test fixtures is exactly what this document exists to prevent:

| Test | Kind of change | Assertion changes? |
|---|---|---|
| `TestCodeownersActorVerification::test_verified_when_milestone_applied_by_codeowner_human` (`:476-483`) | Pass `expected_milestone_title` explicitly; extend `_milestoned_event()` (**`:465`** — *corrected in revision 4; revision 3 cited `:466`*) to emit a real `milestone` object | **Yes** — this is the one test whose subject is the changed behaviour |
| `TestProbeRepoMilestone::test_returns_candidate_with_verified_codeowner_actor` (`:337`) | `_make_issue()` (**`:42-46`** — *corrected in revision 4; revision 3 cited `:41-46`*) gains a `milestone` object | No — fixture only |
| `TestProbeRepoMilestone::test_calls_verify_codeowner_actor_not_verify_label_actor` (`:357`) | same | No — fixture only |
| `TestProbeRepoMilestone::test_url_format_correct` (`:377`) | same | No — fixture only |
| `TestProbeRepoMilestone::test_multiple_issues_all_returned` (`:418`) | same | No — fixture only |
| **`TestProbeRepoMilestone::test_skips_issue_with_no_verified_codeowner_actor` (`:318-335`)** | **NEW in revision 4 — AM-28(d). The shared `_make_issue()` fix is the required remedy.** | **No assertion change — and that is the defect.** See below |

> **The sixth is the vacuous one, and it is the most interesting row in this table (AM-28(d), binding).** `:318`'s test is **#1539's own regression test** — its docstring says so: *"#1539: an issue whose needs-ai/milestone assignment was not applied by a verified human CODEOWNER must not be returned as a candidate."* It builds `_make_issue(100, ["needs-ai", "enhancement"])` — **with no milestone object** (verified by me, §0.0.3) — and patches `_verify_codeowner_actor` to return `None`, then asserts `results == []`.
>
> **Under AM-18's retained skip rule, the record is dropped BEFORE `_verify_codeowner_actor` is ever reached.** So `results == []` passes — **for the wrong reason.** The test satisfies this design's replacement acceptance criterion (its assertion is unchanged; only its fixture would change) **while no longer exercising its stated subject at all.** It is a green test that proves nothing about the thing it is named for, and it would stay green through a regression that re-admitted every unauthorized issue.
>
> **The remedy is the shared `_make_issue()` fix, and the word "shared" is load-bearing:** a coder who inlines a milestone object into only the four *failing* fixtures leaves this one vacuous, because it does not fail. **It is the only one of the six that a green test suite will not force you to touch.** Fixing the shared helper fixes all five fixture-only rows at once and is what AM-28(d) binds.
>
> **And the direction of travel is worth noting:** AM-18 endorsed the widening from one test to five as **coverage recovery rather than a tax** — a fixture that emits no milestone object does not represent any response the milestone strategy can receive. The sixth is the same thing at a higher grade: not a wrong fixture that happened not to matter, but a wrong fixture that **silently disarmed the regression test for the issue this entire design exists to close.**

The four `TestProbeRepoMilestone` rows are **not** named by AM-4; I found them in revision 2. They change because of the *retained* "a record with no `milestone` object is skipped" rule, not because of the inversion — the fixtures never carried a `milestone` object, so those records are dropped before `_verify_codeowner_actor` is reached and `mock_verify_codeowner.assert_called_once()` fails. Two things follow. First, revision 1's acceptance criterion was **already false** for those four, independently of any ruling; that is my defect and §1.6 records it as mine. Second, this widens the "tests must change" set beyond the subset the architect named — one test to five.

> **Revision 4: the figure is SIX, not five (AM-28(d)).** The paragraph below is revision 3's, recording AM-18's endorsement of the widening from one to five; **the sixth is endorsed on identical grounds and by the same architect**, and the reasoning below applies to it unchanged. The five-test figure in the text that follows is superseded by the table above.
>
> **The five-test figure is KEPT, and AM-18 confirms and endorses it as coverage recovery rather than a tax.** The architect accepted the widening explicitly and declined to regard it as a cost worth minimising, on grounds worth carrying verbatim in substance: **a `TestProbeRepoMilestone` fixture that emits no milestone object does not represent any response the milestone strategy can receive — it is a wrong fixture that happened not to matter.** Read against §9's panel surface **R2** (*nothing in this repo has ever exercised the field FIND-1 is about*), fixing those four fixtures is not overhead at all: it **closes the test-coverage gap FIND-1 lived in undetected**, and it is **the single most useful line item in the S1 test plan**. The objection recorded in revision 2 as §8/E-7 is **withdrawn** (§1.6, §8/E-7) — not because the widening turned out to be cheap, but because the rule that causes it is a deliberate fail-closed decision whose third pillar (the skip and AM-4's derive-from-record title are one construction) I had not seen.

**The diagnostic is tested here too (AM-18):** `test_probe_skips_issue_with_no_milestone_object` is extended to assert that the skip emits `WARN milestone-object-absent issue=#<n>` on **stderr** — capturing stderr, not stdout, and asserting the return value is unchanged. Since `probe.py` writes nothing to stderr today (§0.0.2), this test is also the pin on a **new output surface**: if `multi_customer.py` turns out to capture or parse `probe.py`'s stderr, the coder escalates rather than silencing the line (§1.6).

**The replacement acceptance criterion** (from §1.6, restated as a check a reviewer can run): every existing assertion either passes unmodified or is changed only in its fixture; every changed test is listed in the PR body with the sentence *"this test changed because the behaviour changed"*; and **any test whose assertion must *weaken* is a defect in the change, not in the test.** The same applies to any test asserting the exact `_run_gh` argument list, which changes when `--paginate` is added.

**Added tests:**
- `test_codeowners_humans_is_the_shared_function` — `probe._codeowners_humans is requester_trust.codeowners_humans`
- `test_verify_codeowner_actor_delegates_to_shared_predicate`
- `test_probe_passes_expected_milestone_title` — the milestone branch passes `issue["milestone"]["title"]`, never `None`
- **`test_probe_milestoned_event_for_a_different_title_does_not_authorize`** — FIND-1/AM-4 at `probe.py`'s own call site, not only at the gate's. This is the test that proves the *shipped* defect is closed in the module consumer deployments inherit, and it is the reason §7.3 routes `_verify_codeowner_actor`'s approvals to re-review rather than letting them stand.
- `test_probe_skips_issue_with_no_milestone_object` — retained behaviour (§1.6 row 4), **deliberate under AM-18 as fail-closed on an invariant violation**, and now asserting the `milestone-object-absent` stderr diagnostic as well as the skip. Revision 2 tested it "because it is binding, not because I agree with it"; revision 3 tests it because the reasoning is on the record and I do agree with it — the skip and AM-4's derive-the-title-from-the-record construction are one design, and dropping either alone leaves an inconsistent one.
- `test_probe_events_fetch_is_bounded_paginated` — the `--paginate` argument list and the 10-page bound (AM-5), including that the bound-hit path returns `None` rather than raising.

---

## 7. Recorded deviations, residuals, and the startup-gap analysis

### 7.1 Named deviations from current behaviour

- **DEV-1 — pull-request records are excluded. APPROVED by AM-12, conditional on one test.** The `issues` REST endpoint returns PRs (they share the number sequence, #1236). `next_candidates.jq` has no `select(.pull_request == null)`; `bootstrap/query_issues.sh --list` and `bin/hos-cron`'s milestone-less gate both do. Without the filter, a bounced worker PR (`needs-ai`, in-milestone, draft) would be run through the *issue* gate, found untrusted, and dropped for want of a CODEOWNER actor — a silent behaviour change either way. The architect's reason for approving is sharper than mine and is worth carrying: leaving PR records in would mean the *issue* gate silently adjudicates PR eligibility, which contradicts AD-14's scope boundary far more than filtering them does. **The condition has teeth:** §6.2's `test_bounced_draft_pr_still_reaches_the_worker_and_blocks_the_skip` must demonstrate that a bounced draft PR carrying the dispatch label is still surfaced through `bin/hos-cron`'s PR path (`:1085-1099`) and still prevents the `#1395` no-actionable-work early skip, **with the candidate list empty**. If that test cannot be made to pass, **DEV-1 is withdrawn and PR records stay in** — escalate to the architect. Do **not** adjust the skip logic to make it pass; that repairs the gate's blast radius by widening it.
- **DEV-2 — `2>/dev/null` removed from the `bin/hos-cron` candidates call. REQUIRED, not optional (AM-13 point 1).** Revision 1 filed this as "logging only", which was true and beside the point. It is no control-flow change *in the gate*, and it is the **carrier** for AM-13's `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` block (§2.3 Step F, §2.4): with `2>/dev/null` in place, the one signal distinguishing "every candidate is held for a human" from "the milestone is empty" is written to a stream nobody reads, and the most likely operational failure of this whole mechanism becomes invisible for as long as nobody looks. A fail-closed gate that says nothing is indistinguishable from an empty backlog.
- **DEV-3 — `probe.py`'s `bot_accounts` default becomes file-baseline ∪ env** instead of env-only. Strictly stricter; identical when the env is set, as it is in every current invocation.
- **OBS-1 — `probe.py` does not gain `requester_verdict`.** Its milestone strategy already requires a verified CODEOWNER actor for *every* issue, which is stricter than the gate's trusted-author fast path. Adding requester-trust there could only widen it. Consequence worth recording: in a consumer deployment, a worker-filed `[BLOCKED]` issue is **not** auto-selectable through `probe.py` — existing behaviour, unchanged here.
- **OBS-2 — `probe.py`'s milestone strategy applies no `needs-human` exclusion.** Unchanged by S1/S2, and the reason the marker table's non-live entries are not dead weight (§1.5.1(iii)).
- **DEV-4 — NEW in revision 4 (AM-26). `/approve` stops writing labels, and `machine-accounts.env` becomes a precondition for work selection.** Two consumer-visible behaviour changes that revision 3 did not carry as deviations, because one did not exist yet and the other was never noticed.
  - **`/approve` no longer performs the `needs-human` → `needs-ai` swap** (§2.7.2, AM-25). This is **user-visible behaviour for the one human this system has**, so it is disclosed at H1 rather than absorbed — and the human may decline it, in which case the wording-only mitigation is taken instead. The security properties are identical either way.
  - **After S2, an absent or empty `scripts/framework/machine-accounts.env` stops work selection entirely** (Step B1/B3 → exit 2). **Today it does not**: that file is not shipped, not generated by the installer, and is needed only by the merge gates — so a consumer can and does run cron cycles without it. It fails closed, it is visible via DEV-2, and it is **a real change to the consumer contract that no document stated** until AM-26. §4.3 gives it two homes (the S2 work item and the PR body's upgrade note), because a precondition discovered by watching a worker go quiet is a precondition that was not documented.
- **DEV-5 — NEW in revision 4 (AM-26). The gate's per-cycle GitHub API cost rises from ≈26 to ≈105 requests.** Recorded as a named deviation rather than left in §2.2.1's prose, because it is a change to the **operating envelope** of a path that runs every five minutes, and because AM-19 routes it to the human explicitly. ≈1,260 requests/hour against a 5,000/hour installation limit; **the worst case is the normal case while the queue drains**, which is precisely the post-merge state AM-14's lazy drain creates. **Provenance: derived, not measured** — see §2.2.1, and §8/E-8 on the half of the derivation I cannot reconcile.

### 7.2 Residuals accepted for S2

- **R-1 (ADR-named, required to be recorded).** An untrusted author can edit the issue title or body **after** a CODEOWNER authorizes it. S2's authorization binds to the issue, not to its content. **Closed by S3** (AD-5's digest binding). Strictly smaller than today's "no check at all."
- **R-2.** AD-4 accepts a CODEOWNER `labeled`/`milestoned` event from any point in the issue's history, including one that predates the content's current form. Same class as R-1; closed by S3's invariant 4 (approval newer than the assessment).
- **R-3 — ACCEPTED for S2 by AM-8, CLOSED in S3, and the closing rule is stated now so S3 does not redesign it.** A CODEOWNER `milestoned` event that was later reversed (`demilestoned`) and re-applied by a bot to the same milestone still authorizes — `unlabeled`/`demilestoned` events are ignored in S2. FIND-1's title match closes the *different-milestone* case but not reverse-and-re-apply. The architect's reason for not folding it into S2 is the availability risk, not the security judgment: with AM-4's title binding in place the surviving case requires the human to have genuinely placed the issue in the **work** milestone at some point — materially weaker than FIND-1, where the human's act meant the opposite — while honouring reversal events changes *when* authorization evaporates, and nobody has measured how often bots toggle labels and milestones in normal operation. Getting that wrong stalls all autonomous work behind a human re-click. **Binding for S3, verbatim from AM-8:**

  > Authorization from an event source is evaluated against the issue's **current** state of that signal: a `demilestoned` (for the matching title) or `unlabeled` (for the matching label) event **clears** any authorization derived from a prior matching event, regardless of which actor performed the reversal. Re-establishment requires a fresh qualifying event by a verified human CODEOWNER. A bot re-applying a signal a human once applied does not restore the human's authorization.

  S3 must **measure the availability impact** (how many live issues the rule would de-authorize on the day it ships) before enabling it, and report it in the PR body. One interaction worth recording now, because it is easy to miss when S3 is written: §2.7.4's cutover route — the human removes and re-adds the dispatch label from their own account — **survives this rule**, because the re-add is itself "a fresh qualifying event by a verified human CODEOWNER". The cutover work is not invalidated by S3.

  > **Reachability argument and re-check trigger — NEW in revision 4 (AM-28(f), binding on R-3's S3 entry).** The panel verified twice, independently, and the architect verified the PR-side half a third time, that **the only site adding the dispatch label to an *existing* issue was `label-swap.yml:74` (`/approve`, human-triggered)**; `merge_authority.py`'s dispatch-label writes (`:633` on the oversight-verdict path, `:1223` in `record_pr_bounce`'s default) are **PR-side**, and `docs/LABELS.md:29` documents them as such. **AM-25 strengthens this from "currently unreachable" to "no scripted writer exists at all":** with `/approve`'s label write removed, **the last scripted writer of the dispatch label onto an existing issue is gone.**
  >
  > **The qualifier *"in this repo, today"* is exactly what §9's decay warning is about, so the re-check trigger is stated rather than left implicit: any new automation that writes the dispatch label to a milestoned issue re-opens R-3**, and nothing mechanical detects it. That is the same question §9 puts to the panel as a conformance-test problem — a rule of the form *"no present or future automation adds this label to a milestoned issue"* is a property of the whole repo and is strictly harder to pin than a call site. **This argument and this trigger MUST travel into R-3's S3 entry**, not only live here: an acceptance whose reachability argument is left behind in a superseded design document is an acceptance the next reader has to re-derive or take on faith.
- **R-4 — ACCEPTED by AM-7 with three named revisit triggers.** Marker forgery by an induced bot session (§1.5.2 D-4). Requires an LLM under a trusted App identity to reproduce a deterministic template byte-for-byte *and* apply the target milestone *and* apply the dispatch label — and on this repo exactly one machine-filing site can reach the candidate set at all (§1.5.1(iii)). Bounded, and strictly smaller than today's no-check-at-all. **Any one of D-6's three triggers reopens the separate-App question (§8/E-2) as a required decision, not an option**, and §6.1's `test_marker_table_literals_exist_in_emitting_files` and `test_issue_creation_site_count_is_pinned` are what make triggers 2 and 3 detectable rather than noticed-later. Those two tests are **binding** (AM-7): they are the enforcement arm of a declined structural change, and relaxing either as "brittle string tests" silently converts an accepted residual into an unbounded one.
- **R-5.** The 10-page (1000-event) pagination bound means an extraordinarily churned issue can never be authorized. Availability only, fails closed. **AM-5 adds the part that keeps it a residual rather than a permanent invisible stall:** hitting the bound MUST emit `WARN events-page-bound-reached issue=#<n>` to stderr (§1.4, §2.3 D5.3, F14). Without that line, R-5 lands on precisely this repo's most-relabelled — and therefore most important — issues, with no signal anywhere that it has.
- **~~R-6~~ — CLOSED in revision 4 by AM-22 / AF-8. It is NOT carried, and the closure is the point rather than a housekeeping note.** Revision 3 recorded it as *"the 100-issue single-page list ceiling is preserved from today; a milestone with more than 100 open dispatch-labelled issues truncates silently. Unchanged, recorded, not fixed here."* **Every word of that was true and it was still the wrong disposition**, for a reason nobody measured: **the ceiling was three records from binding.** 97 non-PR records on a 100-record page (measured by the architect 2026-09-15, re-derived by me 2026-09-16 — §0.0.3), moving toward the edge **monotonically** as new issues are filed, and dropping **the oldest** — which is exactly the region P-1 starved. **A P-1 fix that changed the cap and left the page would have fixed the instance and left the class**, and the failure would have returned in weeks with an identical user-visible signature (*#1539 is authorized and never gets built*), a different cause, and no diagnostic. **Step C now paginates at 5 pages / 500 records** (§2.2.1), the truncation case is **F33** with the loudest report class, and reaching it is an operational emergency rather than a residual. **The lesson recorded against this row, because it generalises past it: a residual recorded without its distance to firing is a residual nobody can prioritise.** R-6 was honestly recorded for three revisions; what nobody did was run the one query that would have shown it was about to bind.
- **R-7 — NEW, added by AM-4.** Renaming a milestone **de-authorizes every issue authorized under the old title** until a human re-acts. Fail-closed, and an availability cost only. It is the accepted price of byte-for-byte title equality (F28/F29), and the correct trade against prefix-matching, which this repo's own em-dashed, version-prefixed milestone titles would make dangerously loose — `bootstrap/edit_issue.sh:149-169` prefix-matches milestones **by design**, and the gate must not copy it (§1.4). Two consequences worth stating rather than discovering: the de-authorization is silent from the issue's point of view (it simply stops being selected), so a milestone rename during an active build week looks exactly like the gate holding everything — which is what AM-13's `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` line (F30) exists to disambiguate; and the human's re-act is subject to §2.7.4's already-applied-signal problem, because a renamed milestone's issues still carry the dispatch label.

**Recorded in the S2 work item — which is `#1539` (AM-27 point 3; NEW in revision 4).** The original ADR §3 carries a standing requirement that named residuals be written into the work item. **R-1** (ADR-named), **R-3** (AM-8: "recorded in the S2 work item alongside R-1") and **R-7** (AM-4: "to be recorded in the S2 work item") are the three under that requirement.

> **Revision 3 named "the S2 work item" as the carrier of three residuals and never identified it.** The panel is right that a binding instruction with no named landing site lands nowhere. **It is `#1539`**, subject to **H6**'s confirmation that S2 is the fix that closes it — already the architect's recommendation in ESC-1 and the panel's own finding that *"S2 subsumes the Step 0 self-triage half"*. **Binding: when H6 is answered affirmatively, R-1, R-3 (with AM-28(f)'s reachability argument and re-check trigger attached) and R-7 are posted as a comment on #1539**, together with AM-26's `machine-accounts.env` consumer precondition. **If H6 comes back negatively, the work item is whatever issue the human names and the same obligation follows it.** Filing and commenting are worker actions, not this document's — but *that* they happen, and *where*, is binding.
>
> **R-1's weight is raised, not merely carried** (`REQUIREMENTS-1540-AMENDMENT-1` AR-6, ratified by AM-23 point 3). With FR4 narrowed to trust and the authorization model governing, **R-1 is now a residual on the *only* live barrier**: an untrusted author can edit the issue's title or body after a CODEOWNER authorizes it, and S2 will not notice. It reaches the human directly in **H1 item 5**, in pm-agent's words: *"what you read is not guaranteed to be what gets built."* That is a change in significance without a change in substance, and it is the kind of change that is easy to miss when a residual's text stays the same.

**R-2, R-4 and R-5** are recorded here and carried by this document; they are not separately required in the work item, and I have not padded the list to look thorough. **R-6 is closed** (above) and is therefore carried nowhere.

### 7.3 Startup-gap recovery and affected-sign-offs analysis

FIND-1 and FIND-2 are defects in code that shipped for #1539 (`abef062e`) and that S2 now depends on. Asking the required question — *should this have been settled before code was written against it?* — the answer is **yes**: #1539 went from human ruling to merged code with **no requirements and no technical-design stage**, so the contract `_verify_codeowner_actor` had to meet was never written down anywhere, and two defects (FIND-1 HIGH, FIND-2 LOW) plus one unstated impossibility (AF-5) were found by the first design document to look at it, five days later.

The architect reached the same conclusion independently in the amendment's §2 and **widened it**: the gap covers **AM-4, AM-6 and AM-10** — three instances of one class, an ADR asserting a mechanism without checking it against the mechanism's own constraints. AM-6's instance is the sharpest: AD-4 said "events **and comments**" and AD-6 assumed `/approve` was reusable as an authorization channel, and a five-minute read of `label-swap.yml`'s `env:` block — which AD-6 already cites for three other properties — would have caught it. AM-10's is a contradiction *inside one decision* (`ADR-1604` AD-9 points 1 and 2).

**One `startup-artifact-gap` issue covering AM-4 / AM-6 / AM-10 is required**, and it is **Amendment 1 §3(a) item A11 — a worker action, explicitly not a gate on the build.** Neither the architect nor I have filed it: the architect does not open issues from an architecture ruling, and this task is design-document-only. It is recorded here so that the absence of the issue is a visible omission rather than a silent one.

**Updated in revision 3 (Amendment 2 §2 and §3(a) A18), still a worker action and still not a gate.** The issue was filed **twice** — **#1692 and #1693 are the same issue** — so one should be closed. The survivor must be **annotated**, not supplemented with new issues, with three further instances of the *same* root cause (a load-bearing claim asserted rather than derived, in a document whose job is to be the thing others rely on): **AM-14/AF-6** (the "far smaller than 46" volume claim, wrong by measurement, asserted in the same document whose §3 flagged that exact defect class); **AM-17/E-6** (Amendment 1 contradicting itself about `.github/workflows/**` in two sections, caught by reading the document against itself); and **AF-7** (the architect's first reading of the duplicate-`labeled` scan overstating a blind spot that measurement showed to be empty — the same defect with the opposite sign, and understating evidence is not the safe direction, because it misleads H1 and misdirects the panel just as surely). **Nothing was built or designed against any of the three**, so nothing is orphaned by them; the analysis is in §7.3's sign-off table above. A separate, narrower follow-up is also requested by AF-7 and is **explicitly not folded into S2**: land the paginated duplicate-`labeled`-event scan in `scripts/` with a test, per `CLAUDE.md`'s "the second time you need it" rule, so the panel and any consumer deployment can re-run what they are handed. S2 must not acquire a sixth thing to carry.

**Updated in revision 4 (Amendment 3 §2 and §3(a) A30) — still a worker action, still not a gate, and still an ANNOTATION rather than a new filing.** Amendment 3 asks that the surviving `startup-artifact-gap` issue (#1692/#1693, one to be closed) be annotated with **four further instances**, and they are worth listing with their *shapes* rather than their names, because the shapes are what recur:

1. **AM-19 / AM-22 / AF-8 — a load-bearing number asserted rather than derived.** The authorization-check cap was introduced by *this document*, at self-rated MEDIUM confidence, explicitly "not derived from measurement" — and it reached the human's clearance text **inside an assurance it falsified**. One `--list` query, which the architect ran twice in under a minute and which I ran again this session, was the whole of the missing evidence. **The same one-command omission produced AF-6 and AF-8.** The architect records the architecture half as theirs (AD-3 bound the gate's algorithm and never asked what bounds its cost or in what order it spends it); **the design half is mine**, and the honest statement is that I introduced a bound whose value decided whether a `priority:critical` issue could ever be selected, and did not measure the population it applied to.
2. **AM-23 / P-2 — a claim correct in the place nobody reads and wrong in the place the decision-maker reads.** AD-4's header asserted FR4 compliance while its body specified the opposite of FR4 as written, 36 lines apart in one document. **The architect records this as the third instance of that class in this chain** (AM-17's four-versus-five was the first, P-7(b)'s uncorrected BLAST RADIUS line the second). **It has a design half too, and it is AR-5's finding: FR4 appeared zero times in revision 3's 1,363 lines.** A design that implements a requirement without naming it is one revision away from implementing its opposite without noticing — which is exactly what happened.
3. **AM-25 — accepting a stated justification without checking it, in a document that records the acceptance as a ruling.** I stated three reasons for leaving `/approve` live; the architect read them, found them sound, and declined to overrule. **Two were false and both were checkable in one `grep` each.** Amendment 2 §4 records "the architect read the reasons" as though that were verification. **It was not — and the half that is mine is worse: I wrote two of them without checking either**, and both were false in the direction that supported the conclusion I had already reached (§2.7.2).
4. **AF-9 — an internal contradiction inside one ADR section.** AD-3 says an untrusted candidate costs one events call *"only until it is approved or declined"*; AD-4, in the same section, says eligibility is **never cached** and is re-verified every cycle. Both cannot be true, and **the cost model that P-1's fix turns on was the one that was wrong.** Nothing was built on it, but H1's cost disclosure would have been wrong had the architect not caught it.

**Do not file new issues for these** — Amendment 3 §3(a) A30 is explicit. **And the §4 follow-ups Amendment 3 recommends are recommendations to the human, not filings** (filing is the intake path, and #1539's whole subject is what may enter it). The five it names: `scripts/run_spec_panel.sh` does not exist despite ADR-033 AD-2 binding it as the single deterministic invocation site for **this very gate** — *"D41's own failure class inside the mechanism built to catch it"*, and it has a cost already paid, because **this panel must now re-run and there is no script to re-run it with**; agy's empty-`SUCCESS` envelope hazard; FIND-2's unfixed `_verify_label_actor` half, correctly out of S2's scope but diagnosed and left live; **the `CLAUDE.md` / `bin/hos-cron:1764` `bypassPermissions` contradiction (§2.5)**; and **`query_issues.sh` emitting no `.user` field**, which is why P-1's central authorship measurement is not re-derivable by any later reviewer through the sanctioned read path (§0.0.3).

### Affected sign-offs — which stand, which are orphaned

Revision 1 got the second row of this table wrong, and Amendment 1 §2 corrected it. The correction is recorded as a correction, not smoothed into a fresh table.

> **Revision 4: this table is UNCHANGED AGAIN, and Amendment 3 §2 independently re-ran the analysis over AM-19 … AM-28 and confirmed every row.** Four dispositions are worth stating explicitly, because a reader might reasonably expect a CRITICAL finding and an overruled disposition to have orphaned something.
> (i) **AM-19's algorithm change orphans nothing** — no code exists. `coder` has never been cleared, and revision 3 *"is not a sign-off; it is the input to a panel that returned DO-NOT-BUILD."* **There is no approval to orphan.**
> (ii) **AM-23's adoption of pm-agent's FR4 ruling orphans nothing either, and the reason is precise rather than lucky:** AR-4 establishes that **D5 does not narrow**, so no design and no code changes shape under the ruling. A requirements narrowing that changes no mechanism invalidates no review of a mechanism.
> (iii) **AM-28(d) adds one `test_probe.py` fixture to an *existing* re-review's scope.** `abef062e`'s `_verify_codeowner_actor` approvals remain **ORPHANED / RE-REVIEW REQUIRED** for the milestone path exactly as AM-4 left them; the scope of that re-review is now **six tests, not five**.
> (iv) **H1 has still not been answered, and for the third consecutive amendment that is the only reason nothing is orphaned.** Had H1 been cleared before the panel ran, **the human would have cleared a design in which #1539 — the `priority:critical` issue the whole mechanism exists to close — was permanently unbuildable**, on the strength of an assurance (*"the queue's true size is knowable only by running the gate"*) that was false as designed. **That is luck rather than process, and it is worth saying so plainly: the reason this chain keeps getting away with late corrections is that one human has not had time to answer H1.** I record it here in the design, and not only in the architect's amendment, because the next late correction may not be so lucky and this table is where a reviewer will look for the pattern.
>
> **Revision 3: this table is UNCHANGED, and that is a finding rather than an omission.** Amendment 2 §2 re-ran the affected-sign-offs analysis over AM-14 … AM-18 and confirmed every row below without altering one: **no ruling in Amendment 2 changes an authorization semantic, a trust boundary, or a line of shipped behaviour**, so nothing is newly orphaned and nothing previously orphaned is restored. `abef062e`'s `_verify_codeowner_actor` approvals remain **ORPHANED / RE-REVIEW REQUIRED** for the milestone path exactly as AM-4 left them — AM-18 *confirms* that path's contract rather than changing it, and adds only an observational diagnostic, which cannot orphan an approval because it changes no decision. Two dispositions are worth stating explicitly because a reader might expect them to have moved: (i) **AM-17's fifth protected surface orphans nothing** — no code has been approved for `label-swap.yml`'s confirmation body, and the count is what the *human* is asked to clear, which is Amendment 2 §3(b)'s job, not a sign-off's; (ii) **H1 itself has still not been answered, and that is the only reason nothing is orphaned by AM-14/AM-17** — had it been answered from Amendment 1's text, the human would have cleared a four-surface change set at the wrong stated cost, and that clearance would have had to be invalidated and re-asked. The near-miss is recorded here, not smoothed over, because it is the concrete consequence E-6 prevented. No `code-reviewer` or `security-reviewer` sign-off exists on S1 or S2 code: `coder` is not cleared and no code has been written.

The correction note is a **required element of the S1 change set**, not a nicety — a decision log that overstates what a security fix achieved is the artefact a later reader trusts instead of re-reading the code. | No (confirmed by the amendment) |

**Why `_verify_codeowner_actor`'s approvals are orphaned, stated plainly because revision 1 argued the opposite.** Revision 1 recorded them as *"stand for what they approved — with `expected_milestone_title=None` the shipped behaviour is bit-for-bit preserved and the nine existing tests pass unmodified; the contract is extended, not changed."* **Both halves of that justification are withdrawn.** AM-4 inverts the default, so `None` now means "ignore `milestoned` events entirely" and the milestone path's behaviour **genuinely changes**; and §1.6/§6.4 show five existing tests must change, so "pass unmodified" was false on its own terms — for four of them independently of the ruling. Behaviour-preservation was the thing that made the old approvals transferable, and behaviour-preservation is exactly what the ruling removed. The approvals are therefore orphaned **for the milestone-authorization path**, and re-review is routed to `code-reviewer` and `security-reviewer` at S1.

**Reviewer independence note:** these are re-reviews of *shipped* code against an amended contract, not re-approvals of the same diff. The reviewer must read `probe.py`'s milestone strategy as it will exist after S1 — with the inverted default, the title binding, and the bounded pagination — and must not treat the `abef062e` approval as a starting point to be confirmed.

**The honest closing statement, corrected.** Revision 1 wrote *"the only behavioural change on an approved path is DEV-3 (strictly stricter)."* **That is no longer true.** AM-4 changes `probe.py`'s milestone-strategy authorization semantics on a path that shipped, was approved, and that **every consumer deployment inherits** — the change is strictly stricter in the direction that matters (a forgotten argument now gates rather than authorizes), but "stricter" is not "unchanged", and a consumer running the milestone strategy will see issues stop being selected. What remains true is the property the CORE contract actually requires: **no code approved against the superseded contract is left unaudited against the fix.** The one shipped path this amendment changes is routed to named re-reviewers in the table above, and §6.4 names the tests that will demonstrate the change rather than hide it.
